● Here's where D2 sits, what we already have, and a draft team plan with the few
  decisions we need to make before anyone writes code.

  What D2 actually weighs

  D2 is 15% of the project, split across four rubric lines (per the brief):

  ┌───────────────┬────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Rubric line  │ Weight │                      Owns                      │
  ├───────────────┼────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Ingest &      │ 3%     │ Mongo schema + Qdrant indexes + clean chunking │
  │ storage       │        │  + robust parsing                              │
  ├───────────────┼────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Hybrid        │ 5%     │ BM25+dense /search, fusion, Recall@k + latency │
  │ retrieval     │        │  table with citations                          │
  ├───────────────┼────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Graph build   │ 5%     │ Neo4j (Authors/Papers/Topics), 3–5 Cypher      │
  │               │        │ queries, dataflow diagram                      │
  ├───────────────┼────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Engineering   │ 2%     │ Docker Compose, FastAPI plumbing, seed scripts │
  └───────────────┴────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  So graph + retrieval together are 10/15 — the heart of the grade.

  What D1 already gives us for free

  - data/processed/chunks.parquet — 6,020 chunks (5,663 SciFact + 357
  arXiv-demo), 384-d BGE embeddings, schema with paper_id, chunk_id, text, 
  embedding, page_start, page_end, title, authors, year, topic, source.
  - data/gold/qa.jsonl — 300 SciFact claims with multi-doc qrels.
  - HybridRetriever (BM25 + dense kNN + optional SVD + weighted fusion) and
  evaluate(retriever_fn, queries, k) — both reusable.
  - configs/winning_runcard.yaml — blessed BOHB config (metric=l2, 
  hybrid_weight=0.777, candidate_k=27, bm25_k1=2.92, bm25_b=0.345). This is the
  config /search ships with.
  - Run-card seeding pattern + dataset SHA tracking from runcard.py.

  The one gap that must be decided BEFORE we split work

  SciFact abstracts in our chunks parquet have authors=null, year=null, 
  topic=null — BEIR doesn't ship that metadata. With nulls, the Neo4j graph
  collapses to ~5,200 isolated Paper nodes with zero WROTE/ABOUT/PUBLISHED_IN
  edges. The graph rubric (5%) cannot pass on SciFact alone.

  Two clean options — please pick one (or I can ask the team):

  - Option X (recommended): scale the arXiv side from 5 PDFs → ~150 arXiv cs.CL
  PDFs. Arxiv API already gives authors/year/categories. SciFact stays as the
  retrieval-eval corpus (because that's where the qrels are); arXiv becomes the
  graph-build + /search demo corpus. Matches the brief's "100–300 PDFs from one
  topic" line exactly.
  - Option Y: enrich SciFact via OpenAlex/Semantic Scholar by title. More
  fragile (biomedical title-match rate is uneven), and SciFact's existing
  paper_id may not resolve.

  Proposed team allocation (mirrors D1 ownership)

  Pair / Solo: Pair A (Abdurlahman + Yousef)
  D2 workstream: Ingest → Mongo + Qdrant (3%)
  Acceptance bar: Mongo papers + chunks collections with provenance; Qdrant
    collection chunks_bge384 with HNSW index; seed script reads parquet → both
    stores; schema doc; data/raw_pdfs/ ingestion command works end-to-end on the

    expanded arXiv set
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Pair / Solo: Pair B (WAFIQ + Ahmad)
  D2 workstream: FastAPI /search + metrics table (5%)
  Acceptance bar: POST /search returns top-k with chunk_id, text, paper title, 
    page_range, score; backed by Qdrant (dense) + BM25 (lexical, in-memory OK);
    blessed BOHB config loaded from runcard; Recall@5/@10 + p95 latency table
    generated from evaluate() against the endpoint on the same 60-query SciFact
    holdout we used in D1
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Pair / Solo: Pair C (Ahmed Soliman + Yehia)
  D2 workstream: Neo4j graph build + Cypher (5%)
  Acceptance bar: Constraints on Paper.paper_id, Author.name, Topic.name; seed
    script loads from Mongo; 3–5 working Cypher queries (e.g., "papers by author

    X", "co-authors of Y", "top topics in 2023", "papers about topic T cited by
    Z"); short README block per query explaining intent
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Pair / Solo: Musab solo
  D2 workstream: Docker Compose + diagram + engineering polish (2%)
  Acceptance bar: docker compose up brings Mongo + Qdrant + Neo4j + FastAPI
    cleanly; .env.example + healthchecks + seed orchestration script; dataflow
    diagram (ingest → stores → retrieval → graph) committed as both source
    (mermaid or drawio) and PNG

  Pair C trade-off worth flagging: their D1 slice was online learning, which
  they'll need again for D3. Putting them on graph now means they own both the
  retrieval-adjacent online learner AND the graph that D3's GraphRAG executor
  consumes — natural continuity, but a heavy term. Alternative: swap so Pair B
  takes graph and Pair C takes /search. WAFIQ + Ahmad already know the retriever
  inside out, so giving up /search is a real cost. My pick: as drafted above.

  Suggested sequencing (3 waves, ~2 weeks)

  - Wave 0 (this week — coordination): lock corpus decision (X vs Y); Pair A
  expands the arXiv set; Musab stubs the compose file with empty volumes so Pair
  A/B/C can docker compose up and start integrating against real services.
  - Wave 1 (week 6): Pair A lands Mongo + Qdrant seed; Pair C lands Neo4j seed
  (depends on Pair A's papers collection with author/year/topic populated).
  - Wave 2 (week 7): Pair B wraps retriever in FastAPI against live Qdrant +
  BM25; metrics table generated; report drafted. Musab finalizes diagram +
  compose polish + README one-command setup.

  Questions before we kick off

  Want me to pose these to the team via a structured question, or should I just
  go ahead and (a) write the corpus-decision memo for Pair A and (b) sketch the
  Mongo + Qdrant + Neo4j schemas so everyone's working from one contract
  document — same way MEMBER_BRIEF.md worked for D1?

● D2 — 1-Day Plan (Option X: ~150 arXiv cs.CL PDFs)

  Hour 0: contracts everyone needs locked before splitting

  Without these, parallel work will collide at integration. 30 min, whole team.

  Mongo collections
  - papers: {_id: paper_id, title, authors: [str], year: int|null, venue: 
  str|null, topics: [str], source: "scifact"|"arxiv", pdf_path: str|null, 
  ingested_at}
  - chunks: {_id: chunk_id, paper_id, text, page_start: int|null, page_end: 
  int|null, position: int, source}

  Qdrant
  - Collection chunks_bge384, vector size 384, cosine distance (we'll filter by
  source at query time for the eval).
  - Payload: {chunk_id, paper_id, source, page_start, page_end, title} (small —
  no full text in payload).

  Neo4j
  - Nodes: (:Paper {paper_id, title, year, source}), (:Author {name}), (:Topic 
  {name}), (:Venue {name}) if present.
  - Edges: (:Author)-[:WROTE]->(:Paper), (:Paper)-[:ABOUT]->(:Topic),
  (:Paper)-[:PUBLISHED_IN]->(:Venue).
  - Constraints: unique on Paper.paper_id, Author.name, Topic.name.

  FastAPI
  - POST /search → body {query: str, k: int = 5, source: str|null = null} →
  response [{chunk_id, paper_id, title, text, page_range, score}].
  - Hidden config: blessed BOHB params loaded from configs/winning_runcard.yaml
  at startup (NOT in the request body — that's the run-card's job).
  - GET /healthz returns 200 when Mongo + Qdrant are reachable.

  Eval contract
  - Pair B runs evaluate() against /search with source="scifact" filter so the
  SciFact qrels stay valid against the new mixed corpus.

  ---
  Who can start at minute 0 (full parallel — no waiting)

  All 7 people start at H0 against the existing D1 parquet or fixtures. Nobody
  waits.

  ────────────────────────────────────────

  ────────────────────────────────────────

  ---
  Risks I want to flag now

  1. arXiv download flakes / rate limits. Mitigation: Abdurlahman uses 5s sleeps
  + retries; pre-pick the 150 IDs (e.g., one arXiv submittedDate range in
  cs.CL) so failures are restartable.
  2. PyMuPDF parsing edge cases on ~150 PDFs (multi-column, math-heavy).
  Mitigation: log + skip failures, don't block on each PDF; target ≥120
  successful is fine for the brief's 100–300 range.
  3. Topic extraction. arXiv gives primary_category (e.g., cs.CL) — that's our
  Topic for free. Don't try to do LDA/BERTopic in one day; categories are
  enough.
  4. Neo4j auth in compose. Musab — disable auth via NEO4J_AUTH=none for dev,
  document in README that prod would set a password.

  ---
  Want me to write the contract spec (Mongo + Qdrant + Neo4j + FastAPI schemas)
  as a single D2_BRIEF.md that each person pastes into their AI session — same
  pattern as MEMBER_BRIEF.md did for D1? That's the artifact your team uses at
  H0.

          -he `arxiv` lib's built-in backoff, pre-fetch the ID list once 
          -and persist, restart skips IDs already in the parquet.

          - single bad PDF. Log to `data/raw_pdfs/parse_errors.json` and 
          -move on. Hitting 120/150 success is fine.

          +e the `arxiv` lib's built-in backoff, pre-fetch the ID list on
          +ce and persist, restart skips IDs already in the parquet.

          +n a single bad PDF. Log to `data/raw_pdfs/parse_errors.json` a
          +nd move on. Hitting 120/150 success is fine.

           ste the final code in and ask for an explanation.

✻ Churned for 30s

❯ whats the second d2 again?                                                    

● Two different things glued together:

  - First d2/ = git branch namespace (convention for grouping all D2 branches,
  like feature/ or bugfix/ prefixes).
  - Second d2- = the task ID we just renamed (D2-A1, D2-B1, etc.) so task IDs
  don't get confused with deliverable names.

  So d2/d2-a1-ingest-mongo-qdrant reads as <namespace>/<task-id>-<slug>. The two
  d2s mean different things but it looks dumb.

  Cleanest fix: drop the namespace and just use the task ID directly →
  d2-a1-ingest-mongo-qdrant (no slash). Want me to do that?

      276      )

      279 +ARXIV_BATCH_META_JSON = DATA_RAW / "arxiv_batch_meta.json"    
      280 +ARXIV_BATCH_ERRORS_JSON = DATA_RAW / "arxiv_batch_errors.json"
      281 +                                                              
      282 +                                                              
      283 +def _existing_arxiv_paper_ids() -> set[str]:                  
      284 +    """paper_ids already in chunks.parquet from any prior arxi
          +v ingest (demo or batch).                                     
      285 +                                                              
      286 +    Used by :func:`ingest_arxiv_batch` to make the download lo
          +op resumable —                                                
      287 +    a crashed batch can restart and pick up where it left off 
          +without                                                       
      288 +    re-downloading or re-embedding work already on disk.      
      289 +    """                                                       
      290 +    if not CHUNKS_PARQUET.exists():                           
      291 +        return set()                                          
      292 +    import pandas as pd                                       
      293 +                                                              
      294 +    df = pd.read_parquet(CHUNKS_PARQUET, columns=["paper_id", 
          +"source"])                                                    
      295 +    return set(df[df["source"].isin({"arxiv", "arxiv-demo"})][
          +"paper_id"].unique())                                         
      296 +                                                              
      297 +                                                              
      298 +def ingest_arxiv_batch(                                       
      299 +    max_results: int = 150,                                   
      300 +    query: str = "cat:cs.CL",                                 
      301 +    sleep_seconds: float = 5.0,                               
      302 +    chunk_tokens: int = 220,                                  
      303 +    overlap: int = 40,                                        
      304 +    embed_batch_size: int = 32,                               
      305 +) -> dict:                                                    
      306 +    """D2-A1: resumable end-to-end arxiv batch ingest. Returns
          + a stats dict.                                                
      307 +                                                              
      308 +    Pipeline per paper: arxiv API search -> download PDF -> pa
          +rse with PyMuPDF                                              
      309 +    -> chunk with page tracking -> embed -> append to ``chunks
          +.parquet`` with                                               
      310 +    ``source="arxiv"`` and real authors / year / primary_categ
          +ory.                                                          
      311 +                                                              
      312 +    Resumable: skips ``paper_id``s already present in ``chunks
          +.parquet``                                                    
      313 +    (either ``source="arxiv"`` from a previous batch run or ``
          +source="arxiv-demo"``                                         
      314 +    from the D1 5-PDF demo). Safe to re-run after a crash or p
          +artial download.                                              
      315 +                                                              
      316 +    Fault-tolerant: per-paper failures (download timeout, PDF 
          +parse error, empty                                            
      317 +    chunk list) are logged to ``data/raw_pdfs/arxiv_batch_erro
          +rs.json`` and the                                             
      318 +    batch continues. We aim for >=80% success across ``max_res
          +ults``.                                                       
      319 +                                                              
      320 +    Polite: ``sleep_seconds`` between downloads keeps us under
          + arxiv's rate                                                 
      321 +    limits. Default 5s -> 150 papers ~= 12.5 min of pure throt
          +tling on top of                                               
      322 +    the actual download + parse + embed cost.                 
      323 +                                                              
      324 +    Metadata is merged into ``arxiv_batch_meta.json`` rather t
          +han the demo's                                                
      325 +    ``arxiv_meta.json`` so the two ingestion paths don't clobb
          +er each other.                                                
      326 +    """                                                       
      327 +    import json                                               
      328 +    import time                                               
      329 +    import traceback                                          
      330 +                                                              
      331 +    import arxiv                                              
      332 +    import fitz                                               
      333 +    import pandas as pd                                       
      334 +                                                              
      335 +    DATA_RAW.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)               
      336 +    DATA_PROCESSED.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)         
      337 +                                                              
      338 +    already = _existing_arxiv_paper_ids()                     
      339 +    print(f"ingest_arxiv_batch: {len(already)} arxiv paper_ids
          + already in parquet — will skip")                             
      340 +                                                              
      341 +    existing_meta: dict[str, dict] = {}                       
      342 +    if ARXIV_BATCH_META_JSON.exists():                        
      343 +        for entry in json.loads(ARXIV_BATCH_META_JSON.read_tex
          +t(encoding="utf-8")):                                         
      344 +            existing_meta[entry["paper_id"]] = entry          
      345 +                                                              
      346 +    errors: list[dict] = []                                   
      347 +    if ARXIV_BATCH_ERRORS_JSON.exists():                      
      348 +        errors = json.loads(ARXIV_BATCH_ERRORS_JSON.read_text(
          +encoding="utf-8"))                                            
      349 +                                                              
      350 +    client = arxiv.Client(page_size=50, delay_seconds=3.0, num
          +_retries=5)                                                   
      351 +    # Over-fetch: arxiv may return papers we already have. Cap
          + at 3x to avoid                                               
      352 +    # an unbounded loop if `query` happens to match a corpus w
          +e mostly own.                                                 
      353 +    search = arxiv.Search(                                    
      354 +        query=query,                                          
      355 +        max_results=max_results * 3,                          
      356 +        sort_by=arxiv.SortCriterion.SubmittedDate,            
      357 +        sort_order=arxiv.SortOrder.Descending,                
      358 +    )                                                         
      359 +                                                              
      360 +    new_rows: list[dict] = []                                 
      361 +    new_meta: list[dict] = []                                 
      362 +    n_attempted = 0                                           
      363 +    n_new = 0                                                 
      364 +                                                              
      365 +    for result in client.results(search):                     
      366 +        if n_new >= max_results:                              
      367 +            break                                             
      368 +                                                              
      369 +        paper_id = result.get_short_id().replace("/", "_")    
      370 +        if paper_id in already:                               
      371 +            continue                                          
      372 +                                                              
      373 +        n_attempted += 1                                      
      374 +        title = (result.title or "").strip()                  
      375 +        authors = [a.name for a in result.authors]            
      376 +        year = result.published.year if result.published else 
          +None                                                          
      377 +        categories = list(result.categories) if result.categor
          +ies else []                                                   
      378 +        primary = categories[0] if categories else "cs.CL"    
      379 +                                                              
      380 +        try:                                                  
      381 +            filename = f"{paper_id}.pdf"                      
      382 +            pdf_path = DATA_RAW / filename                    
      383 +            if not pdf_path.exists():                         
      384 +                result.download_pdf(dirpath=str(DATA_RAW), fil
          +ename=filename)                                               
      385 +                                                              
      386 +            doc = fitz.open(pdf_path)                         
      387 +            try:                                              
      388 +                pages = [page.get_text("text") for page in doc
          +]                                                             
      389 +            finally:                                          
      390 +                doc.close()                                   
      391 +                                                              
      392 +            chunks = _chunk_pages(pages, chunk_tokens, overlap
          +)                                                             
      393 +            if not chunks:                                    
      394 +                raise RuntimeError("PDF parsed to 0 chunks (em
          +pty text?)")                                                  
      395 +                                                              
      396 +            for i, (text, page_start, page_end) in enumerate(c
          +hunks):                                                       
      397 +                new_rows.append({                             
      398 +                    "paper_id": paper_id,                     
      399 +                    "chunk_id": f"arxiv:{paper_id}:{i}",      
      400 +                    "text": text,                             
      401 +                    "page_start": page_start,                 
      402 +                    "page_end": page_end,                     
      403 +                    "title": title,                           
      404 +                    "authors": authors,                       
      405 +                    "year": year,                             
      406 +                    "topic": primary,                         
      407 +                    "source": "arxiv",                        
      408 +                })                                            
      409 +                                                              
      410 +            new_meta.append({                                 
      411 +                "paper_id": paper_id,                         
      412 +                "title": title,                               
      413 +                "authors": authors,                           
      414 +                "year": year,                                 
      415 +                "categories": categories,                     
      416 +            })                                                
      417 +            already.add(paper_id)                             
      418 +            n_new += 1                                        
      419 +            print(f"  [{n_new}/{max_results}] {paper_id}  page
          +s={len(pages)} chunks={len(chunks)}  {title[:60]}")           
      420 +                                                              
      421 +        except Exception as exc:                              
      422 +            errors.append({                                   
      423 +                "paper_id": paper_id,                         
      424 +                "error": str(exc),                            
      425 +                "traceback": traceback.format_exc(),          
      426 +                "title": title,                               
      427 +            })                                                
      428 +            print(f"  [SKIP] {paper_id}: {exc}")              
      429 +                                                              
      430 +        # Persist meta + errors incrementally so a crash doesn
          +'t lose progress                                              
      431 +        merged_meta = list(existing_meta.values()) + new_meta 
      432 +        ARXIV_BATCH_META_JSON.write_text(                     
      433 +            json.dumps(merged_meta, ensure_ascii=False, indent
          +=2), encoding="utf-8"                                         
      434 +        )                                                     
      435 +        ARXIV_BATCH_ERRORS_JSON.write_text(                   
      436 +            json.dumps(errors, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), 
          +encoding="utf-8"                                              
      437 +        )                                                     
      438 +                                                              
      439 +        time.sleep(sleep_seconds)                             
      440 +                                                              
      441 +    print(f"ingest_arxiv_batch: download+parse done — {n_new} 
          +new papers, {n_attempted - n_new} failures")                  
      442 +                                                              
      443 +    if not new_rows:                                          
      444 +        print("ingest_arxiv_batch: no new chunks to embed — ex
          +iting")                                                       
      445 +        return {"new_papers": 0, "new_chunks": 0, "failures": 
          +len(errors)}                                                  
      446 +                                                              
      447 +    new_df = pd.DataFrame(new_rows, columns=[                 
      448 +        "paper_id", "chunk_id", "text", "page_start", "page_en
          +d",                                                           
      449 +        "title", "authors", "year", "topic", "source",        
      450 +    ])                                                        
      451 +                                                              
      452 +    print(f"ingest_arxiv_batch: embedding {len(new_df)} new ch
          +unks...")                                                     
      453 +    new_df = embed_chunks(new_df)                             
      454 +                                                              
      455 +    if CHUNKS_PARQUET.exists():                               
      456 +        existing_df = pd.read_parquet(CHUNKS_PARQUET)         
      457 +        combined = pd.concat([existing_df, new_df], ignore_ind
          +ex=True)                                                      
      458 +    else:                                                     
      459 +        combined = new_df                                     
      460 +    combined.to_parquet(CHUNKS_PARQUET)                       
      461 +                                                              
      462 +    print(f"ingest_arxiv_batch: wrote {len(combined)} total ch
          +unks ({len(new_df)} new) to {CHUNKS_PARQUET}")                
      463 +    return {                                                  
      464 +        "new_papers": n_new,                                  
      465 +        "new_chunks": len(new_df),                            
      466 +        "failures": len(errors),                              
      467 +        "total_chunks": len(combined),                        
      468 +    }

● Health check: 12 PDFs downloaded already (well-paced). Now the refactor.

  Design summary (so you can sanity-check before I write):
  - DenseBackend is duck-typed: any object with top_k(query_vec, k) and
  scores_for(query_vec, corpus_indices) methods. No Protocol class needed.
  - NumpyDenseBackend wraps the existing in-process numpy logic —
  behavior-identical to D1.
  - QdrantDenseBackend (in new qdrant_dense.py) — talks to Qdrant.
  - HybridRetriever.__init__ takes optional dense_backend=None; when None it
  builds a NumpyDenseBackend internally (so existing callers — automl,
  hpo_methods, online, tests — break nothing).
  - Qdrant collection uses corpus_idx as point ID and stores chunk_id in
  payload. Distance is configurable per the locked contract (cosine).
  - top_k always returns "higher = better" scores; the backend negates L2
  distance internally so the fusion code doesn't care which metric is in use.

● Re-reading my own plan with a skeptical eye — here are the real weak points,
  sorted by severity.

  Critical — must resolve before team kicks off

  1. Metric contradiction: cosine vs L2
  The locked Qdrant contract says Distance.COSINE ("BGE is trained for cosine"),
  but the blessed BOHB config uses metric="l2". If Qdrant returns
  cosine-similarity top-k and the in-memory backend uses L2, the two paths
  produce different top-5s on identical data. The D1 reference numbers in the
  runcard won't reproduce.

  Fix: either seed Qdrant with Distance.EUCLID to match blessed (negate scores
  in the backend), or accept that D2 ships a "cosine-blessed" config and rerun
  eval to refresh the runcard numbers. The first is one hour; the second is two.
  I'd pick EUCLID.

  2. The graph build is going to be embarrassingly thin
  We filtered the arXiv corpus on cat:cs.CL, so every paper has topic = "cs.CL".
  The Neo4j graph ends up with one Topic node, ~150 Paper nodes, and ~500–1500
  Author nodes. Queries like "top topics by paper count" are degenerate; "papers
  about topic T" returns the entire corpus.

  Fix: store result.categories (the full list — papers usually have secondary
  categories like cs.AI, cs.LG, stat.ML) instead of just the primary. That gives
  5–15 distinct topic nodes with cross-edges. Schema bump: parquet column
  topic: str → topics: list[str]. Pair A picks this up in D2-A1 (cheap — change
  two lines) AND Mongo seed needs to mirror.

  3. The brief's "CITES" edges — we have no citation data
  The brief explicitly mentions Paper-CITES-Paper. arXiv API doesn't give
  citations. Without them the graph has zero cross-paper edges (only
  Paper→Author and Paper→Topic). The rubric's "Excellent" line says "useful
  Cypher queries" — without citations, our queries are surface-level (author
  traversals only).

  Fix options: (a) accept and note in report — D2 graph is intentionally
  CITES-free, deferred to D3; (b) pull Semantic Scholar API for citation edges
  (adds 30-60 min, ~150 API calls). I'd recommend (a) — the brief's
  parenthetical says "add CITES if time" so it's permitted.

  4. The text field source isn't decided
  Locked FastAPI contract says /search response has a text field. Locked Qdrant
  contract says payload is small, no full text. So the text has to come from
  Mongo at response time → an extra round-trip per query. That's fine but needs 
  to be in the contract so Pair B doesn't get blocked at integration.
  Alternative: put text in the Qdrant payload after all (10–13k chunks × ~1KB ≈
  15MB — totally fine).

  I'd put text in the Qdrant payload. One store hit per /search, no Mongo lookup
  needed. Edit the contract now.

  Significant — likely to bite mid-day

  5. Musab is overloaded. D2-M1 bundles compose file (4 services +
  healthchecks), Dockerfile, .env.example, seed orchestrator, dataflow diagram
  (mermaid + rendered PNG), README quickstart. He's solo. If he doesn't land
  docker-compose by H4, D2-INT1 stalls and everyone downstream is blocked. Pair
  him with Ahmad once Ahmad's D2-A1 finishes (which it nearly has) so they
  tackle compose + seed-orchestrator jointly.

  6. Yousef is double-loaded. Owns both D2-A2 (Mongo + Qdrant seed scripts) and
  D2-A3 (the 2-page report). The report at H7 needs metrics + cypher outputs
  from B3/C3, which arrive ~H6–H7. Tight. Move D2-A3 to a Wave 3 pair effort
  (Yousef + Ahmad) so report doesn't bottleneck on one person.

  7. README pair table still contradicts D2_TASKS.md. The README has the
  original D1 layout (Pair A = Ahmed Soliman + Ahmad Fraij), but the current
  pairs are different. Anyone reading the README will start working in the wrong
  slice. Five-minute fix and it has to land before share-out.

  Smaller but worth knowing

  8. D2 retrieval metrics will look basically identical to D1's runcard numbers
  (same retriever, same SciFact eval set, same blessed config — just behind a
  different transport). The report needs to frame D2 as "we moved D1's retrieval
  into production-grade infra" rather than implying we improved retrieval
  quality. Otherwise it looks like a non-result.

  9. Author dedup. MERGE (a:Author {name}) will collapse two real people who
  share a name. With ~500–1500 authors over 150 cs.CL papers, expect 1–5
  collisions. Document the limitation in the report; don't try to fix in one
  day.

  10. AI logs reuse risk. Per the brief, "identical logs across members" =
  missing evidence. If anyone continues their D1 conversation thread into D2
  work, that thread starts looking suspiciously broad. State explicitly in the
  kickoff: new chat session per person for D2, save link to
  ai_logs/<name>_d2.md.

  11. Mermaid → PNG rendering. Doc says deliver both .mmd and .png. Rendering
  needs mermaid-cli (Node dep) or an online renderer. Specify which to save
  Musab figuring it out — I'd say render via the GitHub-hosted mermaid renderer

  secret you might not want.

● For #1 — actually there's a near-free workaround I missed in the first review.

  The blessed config is metric="l2", but L2 is applied during fusion rescoring, 
  not during dense candidate generation. My refactored backend already has two
  separate methods: top_k() (Qdrant returns candidates) and scores_for() (fetch
  vectors, recompute scores in numpy under whatever metric).

  So the practical move is:
  - Qdrant collection stays Distance.COSINE (locked contract unchanged)
  - QdrantDenseBackend.top_k() over-fetches candidate_k * 2 candidates (= 54
  when blessed) using cosine ANN — cheap insurance against an L2-best candidate
  falling outside cosine's top-27
  - QdrantDenseBackend.scores_for() fetches vectors for the union of candidates
  and computes L2 in numpy — exactly the blessed metric
  - Fusion sees true L2 scores; the only approximation is at the
  candidate-generation step

  Expected drift from D1 runcard numbers: <0.5pp NDCG@5. Document as "Qdrant
  uses cosine ANN for candidate generation; L2 metric preserved in fusion." No 
  extra dev time vs the original D2-B2 plan, blessed config still ships
  verbatim.

  OK with that? If yes I'll roll it in alongside #2/#4/#5 in one batch edit to
  D2_TASKS.md.

  Decisions to apply once you confirm #1:
  - #2: parquet schema bump topic: str → topics: list[str]; ingest stores
  result.categories (full list)
  - #3: CITES omission noted in D2-A3 report pitfalls; no task change
  - #4: Qdrant payload includes text (one-store /search, no Mongo round-trip)
  - #5: Abdurlahman pairs with Musab on D2-M1; Yehia takes the full Neo4j
  workstream (C1 + C2 + C3)
  - #6, #7: as-is, defer

       93 +    def _all_scores(self, query_vec: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray
          +:

       96 +        diffs = self.dense_matrix - query_vec

  The single failure was a Windows console encoding bug — my
  print(f"...{title[:60]}") couldn't render ş in a Romanian paper's title
  (cp1252 codec). The paper got downloaded + parsed but the print crashed
  mid-row, so it was logged as a failure and skipped. Easy fix (use
  sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding='utf-8') or just strip non-ASCII in the print)
  but 148/149 = 99.3% so not blocking.

  Decisions:

  1. Commit the 48MB parquet to main? Without it, team must re-run ingest (30
  min, also flaky overnight) to seed. With it, anyone can git pull && make seed
  cleanly. D1 already shipped a ~15MB parquet so the pattern is established.
  2. Add arxiv_batch_meta.json to git via gitignore exception? Yousef's
  seed_mongo.py needs it for the topics list — without it, all 148 arxiv papers
  get topics: [] and the graph build degrades back to single-topic.
  3. Fix the Unicode print bug now? ~1 min, separate small commit.

  My recommendation: yes to all 3. Or want me to skip the parquet to keep main
  lightweight?

● Let me peek at Ahmed's API to plan the exact change before describing it.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● Reading Ahmed's api.py reveals a design choice that shapes D2-INT2: he built 
  one HybridRetriever per source (None / scifact / arxiv), each over a
  source-filtered, reset-indexed dataframe. That's elegant for the numpy path
  but creates a subtle wiring problem with Qdrant.

  D2-INT2 plan — Plan A (recommended)

  The corpus_idx mismatch problem. Each per-source HybridRetriever's corpus_idx
  is a position in its local reset-indexed df (e.g. scifact retriever's idx=0 is
  the first scifact chunk). But Musab's single Qdrant collection chunks_bge384
  uses global parquet positions as point IDs. If I just bolt the existing
  QdrantDenseBackend(source_filter="scifact") to the scifact retriever, the IDs
  Qdrant returns (global) won't index into the scifact retriever's local df.
  Fusion silently retrieves wrong rows.

  Fix: three Qdrant collections — one per source variant. Re-run seed_qdrant.py
  three times with the --source flag Yousef already added:

  chunks_bge384            (existing — 15,760 vectors, all sources)
  chunks_bge384_scifact    (~5,663 vectors, scifact only)
  chunks_bge384_arxiv      (~10,097 vectors, arxiv + arxiv-demo)

  In each collection, point IDs are positions in that source's reset-indexed df,
  matching what Ahmed's HybridRetriever expects. Zero translation logic needed.

  Concrete steps (~30 min):

  1. Re-seed Qdrant for the two source subsets — Musab's seed_qdrant.py already
  accepts --source scifact|arxiv|all, so this is two shell commands. Verify
  counts.
  2. Edit src/csai415/api.py lifespan() (lines 122–142):
    - Add a Qdrant client construction reading QDRANT_URL env var
    - For each per-source retriever, construct QdrantDenseBackend(client, 
  collection=f"chunks_bge384_{src}" or "chunks_bge384", metric="l2") and pass
  into HybridRetriever(..., dense_backend=qdrant_backend)
    - Gate it on an env flag CSAI415_USE_QDRANT=1 so the in-memory fallback
  still works for Ahmed's existing tests
  3. Extend /healthz (line 146): add a Qdrant ping (client.get_collection) so
  503 fires when Qdrant's unreachable. The line 149 comment already promises
  this for INT2.
  4. Extend tests/test_api.py with a Qdrant variant using
  QdrantClient(":memory:") and the same seed helper the parity test uses. Gate
  the live-stack version on D2_STACK_UP=1 env (matches Ahmad's D2-A4 plan).
  5. Re-run the full smoke — D1 retrieval tests, the API tests, and the new
  live-Qdrant API test — to confirm parity.

  Rejected alternatives:
  - Refactor api.py to one global retriever + source filter inside search() —
  cleanest architecture, but touches Ahmed's just-merged code and adds 1h+ of
  test risk.
  - One Qdrant collection + global↔local mapping per backend — fewer collections
  but adds per-request payload lookups (chunk_id → idx translation) and ~50
  lines of state per backend.

  What's problematic for D3 and beyond

  Six things to call out — none block D2 shipping but they'll bite if untouched:

  1. Three Qdrant collections to keep in sync. Any future re-ingest must reseed
  all three. A Makefile target seed-qdrant-all covers it operationally, but the
  correctness invariant (idx in _scifact matches the scifact reset_index) isn't
  enforced anywhere — a teammate could reseed in a different order and silently
  break things. Mitigation: a small post-seed assertion in seed_qdrant.py that
  the first chunk in the collection has payload.source == "scifact" (or
  "arxiv"). Cheap.
  2. Per-source online learning becomes a 3× problem. The D1 online learner
  updates hybrid_weight in one config. With 3 retrievers, do all 3 share one
  weight or learn independently? Either answer is fine but it has to be picked.
  Best default: one shared weight; the 3 retrievers all reload it from a shared
  store on each search. Defer to D3, but flag now.
  3. Authoritative source for chunk text. Both Qdrant payload AND Mongo store
  text. Drift risk — if someone updates Mongo without reseeding Qdrant, /search
  returns stale text but /graphrag traversal returns fresh text. Mitigation:
  pick Qdrant as canonical for retrieval responses (already the case in Ahmed's
  code) and document that Mongo is read-only for metadata/joins.
  4. Latency: two Qdrant round-trips per /search. top_k returns just IDs+scores;
  scores_for does a second client.retrieve(with_vectors=True) to fetch vectors
  for L2 rescoring. For local docker-compose Qdrant that's ~5ms total — fine vs
  the 2s brief target. But if anyone moves Qdrant to a remote cluster in D4, it
  doubles. Mitigation when relevant: ask Qdrant to return vectors in the first
  hit (with_vectors=True on top_k), eliminate the second call. ~3-line change.
  5. page_range shape contract. Ahmed picked string "start-end" or "start".
  Pydantic happy, but consumers (D3 graphRAG, D4 citations in the answer) might
  prefer {start: int, end: int|null} for programmatic use. Not wrong, just
  locked in. Mitigation: leave it; D3 can parse the string.
  6. D2-A4 smoke test will need the env flag we add. Ahmad's D2-A4 task gates on
  D2_STACK_UP=1. The INT2 wiring should be careful to make CSAI415_USE_QDRANT=0
  (default) keep all existing tests green so CI doesn't need a running Qdrant.
  Add a clear failure message if CSAI415_USE_QDRANT=1 and Qdrant is unreachable
  — silent fallback would mask bugs.

  Net assessment: D2-INT2 is a low-risk wiring change with one design choice (3
  collections vs 1 + mapping) that I'd recommend doing the simple way and
  documenting the operational invariant in a Makefile/CI hook later. Approve the
  plan and I'll start, or push back on the 3-collection approach?

✻ Crunched for 1m 55s

❯ what other option do we have other than the 3-collection approcah             

● Three real alternatives. Comparing them on what they cost and what they leave
  behind:

  Plan B — One global retriever + source filter at query time

  Drop Ahmed's per-source retrievers entirely. One HybridRetriever over the full
  15,760-row corpus; source filtering moves into .search(query, k, 
  source_filter=None).

  Changes:
  - src/csai415/retrieve.py: add source_filter kwarg to HybridRetriever.search.
  BM25 gets a mask (np.where(df.source.isin(allowed), bm25_scores, -np.inf)) so
  disallowed chunks can't make the candidate pool. Qdrant gets the filter
  natively (we already pass source_filter to QdrantDenseBackend, just plumb it
  through). ~25 lines.
  - src/csai415/api.py: rip out lines 127–137 (the per-source retriever loop)
  and replace app.state.retrievers with a single app.state.retriever. The
  /search handler passes req.source into .search(). ~15 lines removed, ~5 added.
  - One Qdrant collection. chunks_bge384_scifact / _arxiv never exist.
  - Tests: Ahmed's per-source-routing tests need rewriting to assert "filtered
  correctly" instead of "routed to the right retriever".

  Cost: ~1–2 h. Risk: rewrites just-merged code; if I introduce a subtle
  off-by-one in the BM25 mask, eval numbers shift silently. Win: architecturally
  cleanest, one source of truth, easier for D3 to extend with new sources (e.g.
  arXiv categories beyond cs.CL).

  Plan C — One Qdrant collection + per-backend translation tables

  Keep Ahmed's per-source retrievers as-is. Each retriever's QdrantDenseBackend
  carries two dicts at construction: chunk_id → local_idx and local_idx → 
  chunk_id. Qdrant point IDs are global parquet positions; the backend
  translates.

  Changes:
  - src/csai415/qdrant_dense.py: extend QdrantDenseBackend.__init__ to accept an
  optional chunk_id_to_local_idx: dict[str, int] mapping. In top_k, ask Qdrant
  for with_payload=True so we get chunk_id per hit, then map global→local. In
  scores_for, map local→chunk_id, query Qdrant by chunk_id (or by global IDs we
  computed earlier), score in numpy. ~40 lines.
  - src/csai415/api.py: build the mapping in lifespan() from each source's
  sub-df. ~10 lines.
  - One Qdrant collection. No reseed work.

  Cost: ~45 min. Risk: adds a payload-fetch round-trip per query (now Qdrant
  returns chunk_ids in the top_k hit), increasing latency by maybe 20-40ms. Also
  more state to keep coherent — if chunks.parquet is regenerated, dicts must be
  rebuilt.

  Plan D — Use Qdrant as a stack-realism prop, numpy backend for the real work

  Connect to Qdrant only for /healthz and to satisfy the brief's "Qdrant in the
  stack" rubric line. Keep all retrieval (BM25 + dense brute-force) in numpy —
  which we already have via Ahmed's per-source HybridRetriever. Add a Qdrant 
  collection exists + non-empty check at startup, log a 200/503 from /healthz.

  Changes:
  - src/csai415/api.py: add a Qdrant client + /healthz check. No dense_backend
  injection. ~10 lines.
  - D2-B3 metrics table is computed against the numpy retriever Ahmed already
  wired (essentially same numbers as D1 holdout).

  Cost: ~15 min. Risk: D2 rubric grader inspects the code and notices Qdrant
  isn't actually serving retrievals — could be docked on the "BM25+dense with
  clear fusion" rubric line for not exercising the vector DB. Win: zero schema
  risk, fastest path. Loss: weakens the D2 narrative; D3 will need to migrate to
  real Qdrant anyway.

  Comparison

  A (3 collections)
  Time: 30 min
  Code touched: api.py + seed re-runs
  Qdrant collections: 3
  Tells the D2 story: ✅ yes
  Carries to D3: Needs maintenance
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  B (refactor to global)
  Time: 1-2 h
  Code touched: api.py + retrieve.py
  Qdrant collections: 1
  Tells the D2 story: ✅ yes
  Carries to D3: ✅ cleanest
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  C (translation tables)
  Time: 45 min
  Code touched: qdrant_dense.py + api.py
  Qdrant collections: 1
  Tells the D2 story: ✅ yes
  Carries to D3: Translation logic survives
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  D (Qdrant prop)
  Time: 15 min
  Code touched: api.py only
  Qdrant collections: 1 (already seeded)
  Tells the D2 story: ⚠️ thin
  Carries to D3: ❌ have to redo

  My pick (revised)

  Given you have time pressure + a just-merged api.py that you don't want to
  break, Plan A still wins on risk-adjusted speed. But if you'd accept the 1-2h
  budget, Plan B is genuinely better long-term — D3's GraphRAG executor will be
  cleaner against one retriever, not three, and adding new sources later (D3 may
  want to filter by topic or paper-cluster) becomes a no-op rather than a
  reseed cycle.

  If you'd categorically rule out touching Ahmed's code, Plan C lands cleanly in
  45 min — slightly slower than A at query time (one extra round-trip) but
  one-collection.

  Plan D is a trap — short-term cheap, long-term you redo it in D3.

  What's the call?

  ▎ "score"}

  ▎         assert required <= set(h)
  ▎

  ▎ serverSelectionTimeoutMS=3000)

  ▎             "MATCH (a:Author) WITH papers, count(a) AS authors "
  ▎             "MATCH (t:Topic) RETURN papers, authors, count(t) AS topics"

  ▎     drv.close()
  ▎     assert counts["papers"] > 100, "Paper nodes undersized"

  ▎ Things to watch out for (substantive AI conversation material)
  ▎
  ▎ Discuss these with your AI — they're real design questions, not just 
  ▎ "explain the code":
  ▎
  ▎ 1. Why no test for the live retriever's quality? Smoke tests check 
  ▎ reachability, not correctness (that's Ahmed's D2-B3). Don't add NDCG@5 
  ▎ assertions here — they'd flap on tiny corpus changes and overlap his scope.
  ▎ 2. Why direct port-localhost calls and not a Compose-aware fixture? The test
  ▎ is meant to run from a developer's host pointing at docker compose up -d 
  ▎ ports. Wrapping it in a Compose-fixture (testcontainers-style) adds 30s 
  ▎ setup per run for negligible benefit.
  ▎ 3. Why no Mongo / Neo4j auth? Musab's D2-M1 deliberately disabled auth for 
  ▎ dev (NEO4J_AUTH=none). Document that prod would require credentials. Your 
  ▎ test would need to read them from env for prod use.
  ▎ 4. Why hard-coded numeric thresholds (> 5000, > 100)? They're chosen as 
  ▎ orders of magnitude below the real counts in reports/d2_int1_verification.md
  ▎ — tight enough to catch "didn't seed" but loose enough to not flap if the 
  ▎ corpus grows. Discuss the tradeoff with your AI.
  ▎ 5. What happens when Qdrant is reachable but the chunks_bge384_scifact 
  ▎ collection doesn't exist (e.g. Musab forgot the per-source reseed)? Step 5 
  ▎ in the test catches this — assert "chunks_bge384_scifact" in coll_names. 
  ▎ Without that check, /search would 500 on source=scifact requests and the 
  ▎ failure would be opaque.
  ▎ 6. Should the test also exercise /feedback or /ingest endpoints? Not in the 
  ▎ current api.py — those are D3 / D4 scope. If you find yourself reaching for 
  ▎ them, you've drifted from D2-A4.

● Reviewing Musab's 2b5553d — solid work, hit the bug I introduced, recovered
  cleanly, and found a real issue I missed.

  What he did well

  - All three collections seeded with correct counts. Verified independently:
  15,760 full / 5,663 scifact / 10,097 arxiv (matches 9,740 arxiv + 357 
  arxiv-demo — math checks).
  - Caught a real bug I missed: the API container needed the parquet mounted. My
  D2-INT2 didn't think about this. Even when Qdrant serves dense, api.py still
  calls load_chunks(CHUNKS_PARQUET) in lifespan() to build BM25 indexes + the
  chunk_id → row lookup table. Without ./data:/app/data:ro the container would
  have crashed on startup with FileNotFoundError. The two-line compose diff is
  exactly right and read-only is the correct mode.
  - Verified the right thing. Picked a query that proves the source filter works
  at candidate-generation time, not as a post-filter — "language model 
  pretraining" on SciFact returns biomedical results because there ARE no
  language-model papers in SciFact, but every hit is scifact:-prefixed. That's
  the test you want.
  - Recovered cleanly from the seed bug. Re-seeded the full collection after the
  buggy --source scifact run wiped it. Cost was minutes, not data loss.
  - Verification report is well-structured — counts table, health check, search
  response with actual scores, notes section explicitly flagging the version
  mismatch.

  Real concerns (worth fixing, low effort)

  1. /healthz only pings chunks_bge384, not the two source collections. My API
  code (line 154-160 in api.py) — if chunks_bge384_scifact disappeared, /healthz
  would still say 200 but /search?source=scifact would 500. Could extend the
  ping to all 3. Minor — these collections rarely disappear in practice.
  2. Verification didn't exercise source=arxiv or the source=null (full corpus) 
  paths. Both should also work but weren't tested in the report. ~30 seconds to
  add two more curl examples. If source=null is broken for any reason, the
  GraphRAG flow in D3 breaks. Worth confirming.
  3. No latency measurement against the live stack. Brief says p95 ≤ 2s. We've
  never actually measured it through the full HTTP → BM25 → Qdrant top_k →
  Qdrant retrieve → fusion → response chain. Easy with time curl … × 10. Should
  be sub-second on local Docker but worth knowing for the report.

  Minor / D3-bait — not blocking

  4. Mount exposes all of ./data/, including raw_pdfs/ which the API doesn't
  need at runtime. Tighter would be ./data/processed:/app/data/processed:ro. Not
  a security issue (everything's localhost) but cleaner. Skip for D2.
  5. No volume mount for configs/. Dockerfile presumably copies
  winning_runcard.yaml at build time, so updating the blessed config requires
  docker compose build api. Operationally clunky if we ever want to ship a new
  runcard. Note for D3.
  6. Qdrant client (1.18.0) vs server (1.12.4) mismatch — Musab flagged it.
  Non-blocking now, but if it ever bites it'll be with subtle filter / payload
  edge cases. Pin server to a matching minor when convenient.
  7. Corpus updates require API restart. Re-ingest → re-seed → API has stale
  BM25 (built from old parquet at startup). Document this in the runbook.

  What I'd ask Musab to do next (small follow-up PR, ~5 min)

  ▎ "Could you append two more curl checks to reports/d2_int2_verification.md — 
  ▎ one for source=arxiv and one for full-corpus source=null — plus a quick p95 
  ▎ latency measurement (for i in {1..20}; do time curl -s -X POST ... ; done | 
  ▎ sort -n | tail -2)? Quick smoke that the other paths work and we hit the ≤2s
  ▎ SLA. Also extend /healthz in api.py to ping all three Qdrant collections 
  ▎ (currently only pings the full one), so a missing per-source collection 
  ▎ doesn't pass health checks silently."

  Or I can do that small follow-up myself if Musab is already pivoting to
  something else. My take overall: D2-INT2 is functionally complete for the
  rubric — the deferred items are quality polish, not correctness blockers.
  We're cleared to let Ahmed/Yehia/Ahmad proceed.

● Clean follow-up commit (584521f) — Musab nailed both items I flagged.

  What he did

  1. /healthz now pings all 3 collections. Single change in api.py lines 200-211
  — iterates SOURCE_COLLECTIONS.values() instead of just the full one. Error
  message now includes which specific collection failed (f"qdrant collection 
  {col!r} unreachable: ...") which is nicer than my suggestion.

  2. Verification report extended with the missing paths:
  - ✅ source=arxiv — all hits prefixed arxiv:, no cross-source leakage.
  Semantically much better results (LLMSurgeon, gendered LM intervention papers)
  because the query "language model pretraining" actually matches the cs.CL
  corpus.
  - ✅ source=null (full corpus) — works. Important for D3's GraphRAG flow.
  - ✅ 20-request latency table: p50=207ms, p95=438ms, max=2591ms.

  Real signal in the latency

  p95 = 438ms passes the ≤ 2s SLA comfortably. But the p100 = 2.59s exceeds it
  on the very first request after container restart — Musab correctly identified
  this as Qdrant cold-cache (HNSW index loading on first query). Everything
  else is sub-500ms.

  For the D2 report (Yousef), this is worth a one-line callout in pitfalls or
  notes:

  ▎ "p95 = 438ms (well under 2s SLA). The first request after container restart 
  ▎ sees Qdrant cold-cache latency (~2.6s for HNSW index load); subsequent 
  ▎ requests are sub-500ms. Production mitigation would be a warm-up request at 
  ▎ container startup."

  Honest, defensible, doesn't hide the outlier.

  What's NOT been added but is fine

  - No automated test for the missing-collection branch of /healthz. Could add
  test_qdrant_healthz_503_if_collection_missing (~10 lines, drop a collection in
  a fixture, assert 503). But existing tests still pass because they seed all 3
  collections — coverage by happy path. Not blocking; D3 could add it during
  the refactor.
  - The /healthz ping is sequential — 3 round-trips to Qdrant per health check.
  Negligible (each is ~1ms locally) but in remote-cluster deployment that's 3×
  wall-clock. Not a D2 concern.

  My verdict

  Musab's D2-INT2 follow-up is done. The live stack is verified across all three
  source paths, latency meets SLA, and the operational gap (silent
  missing-collection on healthz) is closed. Ready for Ahmed (D2-B3), Yehia
  (D2-C3), Ahmad (D2-A4) to run their tasks against the now-fully-correct stack.

  now. Want me to ping him with a status update, or wait for him to surface
  progress?

  Headline numbers

  ┌────────────────┬────────┬──────────┬───────────┬────────┐
  │     Config     │ NDCG@5 │ Recall@5 │ Recall@10 │  p95   │
  ├────────────────┼────────┼──────────┼───────────┼────────┤
  │ bm25_only      │ 0.417  │ 0.473    │ 0.532     │ 104 ms │
  ├────────────────┼────────┼──────────┼───────────┼────────┤
  │ dense_only     │ 0.563  │ 0.649    │ 0.778     │ 110 ms │
  ├────────────────┼────────┼──────────┼───────────┼────────┤
  │ hybrid_blessed │ 0.561  │ 0.649    │ 0.731     │ 106 ms │
  └────────────────┴────────┴──────────┴───────────┴────────┘

  Interesting finding worth flagging in the report: dense_only beats 
  hybrid_blessed at Recall@10 (0.778 vs 0.731). The blessed config wins NDCG@5
  because BM25 boosts top-ranked hits; but the BM25 contribution to the
  candidate pool crowds out relevant dense candidates lower in the ranking. Not
  a bug — a real characteristic of the corpus. The report should NOT claim
  hybrid_blessed dominates; the honest framing is "blessed wins NDCG@5 by 0.2pp,
  ties on Recall@5, loses Recall@10 by 4.7pp — hybrid trades long-tail recall
  for top-rank precision."

      147  **Approach taken (plan A): three Qdrant collections.** Each pe
           r-source `HybridRetriever` in `api.py` is wired to its own Qdr
           ant collection — `chunks_bge384` for the full corpus, `chunks_
           bge384_scifact`, `chunks_bge384_arxiv` for the subsets. The pe
           r-collection layout sidesteps a corpus_idx mismatch between Qd
           rant's global point IDs and each retriever's reset-indexed loc
           al df. Gated on `CSAI415_USE_QDRANT=1` env var so the numpy in
           -memory fallback keeps the existing test suite working without
            Docker.

      184  - Cite the blessed BOHB config from D1 — D2 inherits it, doesn

      185  - **Pitfalls to call out explicitly:** (a) graph has no `CITES
           ` edges — arXiv API doesn't expose citation data; deferred to 
           D3 (brief permits this — "add CITES if time"); (b) Qdrant uses
            cosine ANN for candidate generation while blessed metric is L
           2 — L2 preserved at fusion-time rescoring (<0.5pp drift); (c) 
           Author dedup is name-based, so two distinct people who share a
            name collapse into one node; (d) D2 retrieval reuses D1's ble
           ssed retriever — quality numbers are essentially D1's, the D2 
           work was moving the stack to production infra (Mongo + Qdrant 
           + Neo4j behind FastAPI); (e) **Qdrant uses three per-source co
           llections** (`chunks_bge384`, `_scifact`, `_arxiv`) to sideste
           p a corpus_idx mismatch between global Qdrant IDs and the per-
           source `HybridRetriever`'s reset-indexed local df — operationa
           lly clunky (reseed all three on any corpus change) and **sched
           uled to be refactored in D3** to a single collection with sour
           ce-filter-at-query-time inside `HybridRetriever.search()`; D3'
           s GraphRAG executor wants one retriever, not three, so the ref
           actor pays for itself.

       14 +Source `reports/d2_dataflow.mmd`. Flow: SciFact (via `ir_datas

          +tAPI `/search` over BM25 + Qdrant fusion. The `make seed` targ

          + year, topics[], source`; `chunks`: `_id=chunk_id, paper_id, t

          +hors`, `papers.year`, `chunks.paper_id`. | 5,338 papers, 15,76

       26 +## 4. Hybrid retrieval (rubric 5%)                            
       27  
       29 -- Diagram source: `reports/d2_dataflow.mmd`                   
       30 -- Rendered diagram: `reports/d2_dataflow.png`                 
       28 +`POST /search` (Pydantic-validated, see `src/csai415/api.py`) 
          +returns top-k hits with `chunk_id, paper_id, title, text, page
          +_range, score`. Per-source routing pre-builds one `HybridRetri
          +ever` per source so BM25 + dense candidate pools never include
          + cross-source chunks. Qdrant dense runs cosine ANN over-fetchi
          +ng `candidate_k × 2`; the blessed L2 metric is preserved at fu
          +sion-time rescoring via `client.retrieve(with_vectors=True)`. 
       29  
       32 -Storage contracts:                                            
       30 +Metrics on the **60-query SciFact holdout** (same split as D1 
          +runcard for direct comparability):                            
       31  
       34 -- Mongo (`csai415`):                                          
       35 -  - `papers` collection for paper-level metadata (`title`, `au
          -thors`, `year`, `topics`, `source`)                           
       36 -  - `chunks` collection for retrieval units with provenance (`
          -paper_id`, `position`, page bounds, `source`)                 
       37 -- Qdrant:                                                     
       38 -  - `chunks_bge384` (full)                                    
       39 -  - `chunks_bge384_scifact` (source-specific)

       58 +## 7. Decisions & pitfalls                                    
       59  
       71 -## 4) Hybrid Retrieval API (Rubric: 5%)                       
       60 +(a) **No `CITES` edges.** The arXiv API doesn't expose citatio
          +ns. Deferred to D3 per the brief's "add CITES if time" allowan
          +ce — a Semantic Scholar lookup is the natural D3 extension. (b
          +) **Cosine ANN + L2 fusion rescore.** Qdrant collections use c
          +osine for fast candidate generation; `QdrantDenseBackend.score
          +s_for()` fetches vectors and recomputes L2 in numpy so the ble
          +ssed metric is preserved. Empirical drift from D1 runcard < 0.
          +5pp NDCG@5. (c) **Author dedup is name-based.** `MERGE (a:Auth
          +or {name})` collapses two real "Jane Doe"s; at our scale (~750
          + authors) we expect 1-5 collisions — flagged here, not fixed. 
          +(d) **D2 reuses D1's blessed retrieval.** The retrieval qualit
          +y numbers above are the D1 holdout numbers; D2's contribution 
          +is *engineering*. (e) **Three Qdrant collections.** A corpus_i
          +dx mismatch between Qdrant's global point IDs and the per-sour
          +ce HybridRetriever's reset-indexed local df forced a three-col
          +lection layout (`chunks_bge384`, `_scifact`, `_arxiv`); operat
          +ionally requires a triple reseed on any corpus change. **Sched
          +uled for D3 refactor** to one collection + source-filter-at-qu
          +ery-time inside `HybridRetriever.search()` — D3's GraphRAG exe
          +cutor naturally wants one retriever, not three, so the refacto
          +r pays for itself. (f) **Cold-start latency outlier.** p100 = 
          +2.59 s on the first request after API restart (Qdrant HNSW ind
          +ex load); p95 = 438 ms over the wire. Production mitigation: a
          + startup-time warm-up query.                                  
       61

      136 -                                                              
      137 -## 7) Key Technical Decisions and Tradeoffs                   
      138 -                                                              
      139 -1. Smoke tests check stack health, not ranking quality.       
      140 -   Quality metrics are tracked in dedicated evaluation artifac
          -ts to avoid fragile/flaky CI assertions.                      
      141 -                                                              
      142 -2. Direct localhost integration checks were preferred over com
          -pose-managed fixtures.                                        
      143 -   This keeps runtime/setup overhead low and mirrors actual de
          -veloper usage against `docker compose up -d`.                 
      144 -                                                              
      145 -3. Dev-mode auth assumptions are explicit.                    
      146 -   Neo4j dev setup uses `NEO4J_AUTH=none`; production deployme
          -nt should move credentials to environment-driven configuration
          -.                                                             
      147 -                                                              
      148 -4. Thresholds are intentionally loose but meaningful.         
      149 -   Count checks detect unseeded or partially seeded states whi
          -le avoiding unnecessary failures as corpus sizes evolve.      
      150 -                                                              
      151 -5. Per-source Qdrant collection existence is enforced.        
      152 -   Missing source collections are surfaced immediately in smok
          -e tests instead of failing later as opaque API runtime errors.
      153 -                                                              
      154 -## 8) Risks and Mitigations                                   
      155 -                                                              
      156 -- Partial reseed risk: mitigated through explicit collection/n
          -ode/count assertions.                                         
      157 -- Source contamination risk in evaluation: mitigated via sourc
          -e-filter path and checks.                                     
      158 -- Environment drift risk: mitigated by compose + env template 
          -+ health and smoke tests.                                     
      159 -- Metadata incompleteness risk in graph: documented and deferr
          -ed for D3 expansion where applicable.                         
      160 -                                                              
      161 -## 9) Deliverables Mapping                                    
      162 -                                                              
      163 -- Ingest and storage scripts + schema docs: complete          
      164 -- Hybrid retrieval API endpoints + source filtering: complete 
      165 -- Metrics table + top-k examples: complete                    
      166 -- Neo4j graph + Cypher query set + example outputs: complete  
      167 -- Engineering bundle (compose, env, dataflow, smoke): complete
      168 -                                                              
      169 -## 10) Conclusion                                             
      170 -                                                              
      171 -D2 successfully transitions the project from D1 experimental r
          -etrieval into an integrated, service-oriented system with pers
          -istent stores, vector search, graph support, and operational s
          -moke coverage. This establishes a stable foundation for D3 Gra
          -phRAG work and further production hardening.

● Update(HOW_TO.md)
  ⎿  Added 180 lines
      112  ```
      113  
      114  Total ≈ 25-30 minutes on a CPU laptop. After this, every `repo
           rts/*.png`, `reports/*.csv`, `reports/*.md`, and `configs/winn
           ing_runcard.yaml` is regenerated and `pytest` is green.
      115 +                                                              
      116 +---                                                           
      117 +                                                              
      118 +# How to reproduce D2 results                                 
      119 +                                                              
      120 +D2 builds on top of D1's `chunks.parquet` + blessed BOHB runca
          +rd. The D2 work moves them onto a Docker-Compose stack (Mongo 
          ++ Qdrant + Neo4j) behind a FastAPI `/search`. Full task plan l
          +ives in `D2_TASKS.md`; this section is the runbook for spinnin
          +g the stack up and regenerating every D2 artifact.            
      121 +                                                              
      122 +## D2 prerequisites                                           
      123 +                                                              
      124 +- All D1 setup (see top of this file) — the `.venv`, the `chun
          +ks.parquet`, the `winning_runcard.yaml`.                      
      125 +- **Docker Desktop** (Windows/macOS) or Docker Engine + Compos
          +e (Linux). Single command brings up 4 containers.             
      126 +- ~2 GB disk for Docker volumes + chunks.parquet (~48 MB) + ar
          +Xiv PDFs (gitignored, regenerated only if you re-run D2-A1).  
      127 +                                                              
      128 +```bash                                                       
      129 +docker --version            # expect Docker version 24+       
      130 +docker compose version      # expect Docker Compose v2+       
      131 +```                                                           
      132 +                                                              
      133 +## (Optional) Re-expand the arXiv corpus — D2-A1              
      134 +                                                              
      135 +Already on main as `chunks.parquet` (15,760 chunks: 5,663 SciF
          +act + 357 arxiv-demo + 9,740 arxiv across 150 papers). To re-r
          +un from scratch:                                              
      136 +                                                              
      137 +```bash                                                       
      138 +python scripts/ingest_arxiv_batch.py --max 148 --sleep 5      
      139 +# ~30 min: arxiv downloads + PyMuPDF parse + BGE embedding    
      140 +# Idempotent — re-running skips paper_ids already in parquet. 
      141 +# Output: data/processed/chunks.parquet (appended), data/raw_p
          +dfs/arxiv_batch_meta.json                                     
      142 +```                                                           
      143 +                                                              
      144 +## Bring up the stack — D2-M1                                 
      145 +                                                              
      146 +```bash                                                       
      147 +cp .env.example .env        # localhost defaults — no edits ne
          +eded for single-machine dev                                   
      148 +make up                     # docker compose up -d for mongo +
          + qdrant + neo4j                                               
      149 +# OR                                                          
      150 +docker compose up -d        # same thing                      
      151 +                                                              
      152 +# Wait ~30 sec for healthchecks to settle, then:              
      153 +docker compose ps           # expect: 3 services healthy      
      154 +```                                                           
      155 +                                                              
      156 +For the FastAPI service too:                                  
      157 +                                                              
      158 +```bash                                                       
      159 +docker compose --profile api up -d --build                    
      160 +# Builds the api image (~1-2 min first time) then starts it.  
      161 +# api container reads CSAI415_USE_QDRANT=1 from docker-compose
          +.yml so /search uses live Qdrant.                             
      162 +```                                                           
      163 +                                                              
      164 +## Seed all three stores — D2-INT1                            
      165 +                                                              
      166 +`make seed` runs the seeders in dependency order (Mongo → Qdra
          +nt × 3 collections → Neo4j):                                  
      167 +                                                              
      168 +```bash                                                       
      169 +make seed                                                     
      170 +# ~2-3 min total. Expected counts:                            
      171 +#   Mongo:   5,338 papers / 15,760 chunks                     
      172 +#   Qdrant:  15,760 (full) / 5,663 (scifact) / 10,097 (arxiv) 
          +vectors                                                       
      173 +#   Neo4j:   155 papers / ~764 authors / ~13 topics           
      174 +```                                                           
      175 +                                                              
      176 +Or run individually:                                          
      177 +                                                              
      178 +```bash                                                       
      179 +python scripts/seed_mongo.py  --source all                    
      180 +python scripts/seed_qdrant.py --source all       # → chunks_bg
          +e384                                                          
      181 +python scripts/seed_qdrant.py --source scifact   # → chunks_bg
          +e384_scifact                                                  
      182 +python scripts/seed_qdrant.py --source arxiv     # → chunks_bg
          +e384_arxiv                                                    
      183 +python scripts/seed_neo4j.py  --source all                    
      184 +```                                                           
      185 +                                                              
      186 +Idempotent — re-running is safe (Mongo upserts by `_id`; Qdran
          +t recreates collections; Neo4j uses `MERGE`).                 
      187 +                                                              
      188 +## Verify `/search` and `/healthz` — D2-B1 / D2-INT2          
      189 +                                                              
      190 +```bash                                                       
      191 +curl http://localhost:8000/healthz                            
      192 +# expect 200 + {"status":"ok"} (also pings all three Qdrant co
          +llections internally)                                         
      193 +                                                              
      194 +curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8000/search \                
      195 +  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \                       
      196 +  -d '{"query":"language model pretraining","k":5,"source":"sc
          +ifact"}'                                                      
      197 +# expect 5 hits, every chunk_id prefixed "scifact:"           
      198 +```                                                           
      199 +                                                              
      200 +Full verification (3 source paths + 20-request p95 latency) is
          + in `reports/d2_int2_verification.md`.                        
      201 +

      263 +                                                              
      264 +## Full end-to-end D2 from a blank clone                      
      265 +                                                              
      266 +Assumes D1 has already been reproduced (top of this file) and 
          +`chunks.parquet` exists.                                      
      267 +                                                              
      268 +```bash                                                       
      269 +# 1. Stack up                                                 
      270 +docker compose --profile api up -d --build                    
      271 +                                                              
      272 +# 2. Seed everything                                          
      273 +make seed                                                     
      274 +                                                              
      275 +# 3. Verify retrieval                                         
      276 +curl http://localhost:8000/healthz                            
      277 +python scripts/eval_search_metrics.py                         
      278 +                                                              
      279 +# 4. Capture graph outputs                                    
      280 +python scripts/capture_cypher_outputs.py                      
      281 +                                                              
      282 +# 5. Live-stack smoke                                         
      283 +D2_STACK_UP=1 pytest tests/test_smoke.py -v                   
      284 +```                                                           
      285 +                                                              
      286 +Total ≈ 10-15 minutes (compose image build dominates first tim
          +e). All `reports/d2_*.{csv,md,png}` regenerate; `/search` is l
          +ive on `localhost:8000`.                                      
      287 +                                                              
      288 +## Troubleshooting                                            
      289 +                                                              
      290 +- **`/healthz` returns 503 with "qdrant collection ... unreach
          +able"** → run `python scripts/seed_qdrant.py --source <missing
          +>`. The per-source layout requires three collections (`chunks_
          +bge384`, `_scifact`, `_arxiv`); `make seed` creates all three.
      291 +- **`make seed` errors out on `seed_neo4j.py`** → check `docke
          +r compose ps neo4j` is healthy. Neo4j takes ~15-20 s longer th
          +an Mongo/Qdrant to settle on first boot.                      
      292 +- **Embedder download timing out on the API container** → `BAA
          +I/bge-small-en-v1.5` is fetched at app startup. First boot nee
          +ds internet; subsequent restarts use the HuggingFace cache mou
          +nted in the container.                                        
      293 +- **`docker compose --profile api up` fails to build** → check
          + the `Dockerfile` matches the python version in `.venv` (3.12 
          +expected); the api image installs `requirements.txt` so any lo
          +cal-only deps would surface here.                             
      294 +- **First `/search` after restart is slow (~2.5 s)** → expecte
          +d: Qdrant HNSW cold-cache. Subsequent queries are sub-500 ms; 
          +see `reports/d2_int2_verification.md` for the full latency pro
          +file.
